# Current NPU architecture (Phase 0)

Audit date: 2026-11-25. Repository: llama.cpp, socmulator-dev, HEAD 64a072afa5a7a22dec74e94d599ffa4b848c9a20 (initially read from submodule metadata; final read-only git diff and ls-tree checks used the pinned revision). Source references below are repository-relative. This is static source evidence, not a build or hardware validation. External SDK implementations, RTL behavior, DMA coherence on the target, and current FPGA results are UNVERIFIED. The external SDK requirement is explicit in cmake/ggml-gemmini-geometry.cmake:20-28; PoTAL validates an external RTL-generated parameter header in cmake/potal-hw-params.cmake:83-125.

## Graph and backend dispatch

GGML scheduler splits submit through ggml_backend_graph_compute_async at ggml/src/ggml-backend.cpp:1479 and ggml/src/ggml-backend.cpp:1501. The synchronous ggml_backend_graph_compute calls the async entry then synchronizes; the async implementation ultimately invokes backend->iface.graph_compute (ggml/src/ggml-backend.cpp:333-403). Device capability queries route through ggml_backend_dev_supports_op (ggml/src/ggml-backend.cpp:573). Capability acceptance is not evidence of accelerator execution: Gemmini can choose a CPU implementation, and PoTAL's native checked issue API returns NO_HARDWARE (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/dense.cpp:792-841; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:902-940).

| Backend | Capability / graph entry | Dispatch and ownership |
|---|---|---|
| Gemmini | ggml_backend_gemmini_device_supports_op implemented in ops.cpp; graph callback ggml_backend_gemmini_graph_compute | Capability implementation and dtype/route checks ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ops.cpp:2286-2358; interface pointer ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini.cpp:424; graph loop ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini.cpp:111-175; GET_ROWS and MUL_MAT dispatch ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini.cpp:175-228. |
| PoTAL INT8 | potal_device_supports_op -> check_op; potal_graph_compute -> compute_mul_mat | ggml/src/ggml-potal/ggml-potal.cpp:311-406 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/ggml-potal.cpp:481-518; validation/compute contracts ggml/src/ggml-potal/ops.cpp:784-816. |
| PoTAL INT4 | Separate source tree selected only when GGML_POTAL AND GGML_POTAL_W4A4 | ggml/src/CMakeLists.txt:320-329. It builds the same target/API names from precision-specific sources, rather than registering another simultaneously available accelerator. ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/CMakeLists.txt:46-50 and ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/CMakeLists.txt:419-420. |

## Gemmini: concrete linear matmul flow

1. ggml_backend_gemmini_graph_compute sends normal MUL_MAT to ggml_backend_gemmini_mul_mat, and marked attention to attention_execute (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini.cpp:219-228). CUDA RTN and CUDA CPU-exact attention can intercept earlier (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini.cpp:182-208).
2. ggml_backend_gemmini_mul_mat constructs the host invocation and chooses weight representation. FLOAT routes dequantize GGUF weights into FP32 on CPU, then call gemmini_matmul_fp_facade (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ops.cpp:965; ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ops.cpp:1100-1181). Output args point directly at dst->data with tensor strides (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ops.cpp:1907-1917). Integer weight preparation ends before submission (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ops.cpp:1892-1894).
3. Activation preparation uses a reuse-aware quantization closure; the shared quantize_activation resets/validates state and calls act::quantize. The dispatcher selects EXSIA, TENSOR, TOKEN, BLOCK, STRIPE; MXINT forces its block quantizer (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ops.cpp:1487-1506; ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/quants/act/quantize.cpp:21-49; ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/quants/act/dispatch.cpp:150-160). Local adaptation and folding execute on CPU; ordered folding creates the stripe-ready handoff (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/quants/act/exsia/exsia.cpp:956-965 and ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/quants/act/exsia/exsia.cpp:1502-1529).
4. Public matmul options resolve execution and residual policy. Pipeline setup constructs prepare_execution and starts MatmulStripeCollector before quantization; FULL facade dispatch uses MatMul::run_full (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ops.cpp:2030-2057 and ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ops.cpp:2093-2101). Geometry resolves through gemmini_set_tile_ws and make_gemmini_geometry (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/execution.cpp:38-44). The checked shape/outer-count implementation is local (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini-geometry.hpp:77-117); the SDK tiler itself is outside this repository, UNVERIFIED.
5. execute_dense handles FP32 CPU matmul, stored block CPU routes, native matched-width CPU routes, and SDK baseline/HP1 routes (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/dense.cpp:764-880). The accelerator-facing dense boundaries are tiled_matmul_auto_baseline at lines 846/849 and tiled_matmul_auto_im2p at line 867. Despite its name, the latter must not be assumed to mean an IM2P simulator: it is called from the ordinary dense facade and comes from the selected external SDK (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/dense.cpp:20 and ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/dense.cpp:864-867). Its internal encoding/issue/fence/reconstruction chain is UNVERIFIED without that selected SDK.
6. WS residual execution stages native operands, calls external tiled_matmul(..., WS), then accumulates checked INT64 results on CPU (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/residual/rmd/rmd-executor.cpp:1259-1315). execute_rmd_stripe and compose_rmd_stripe belong to the execution layer; finalization/finish belong to completion and output management (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/execution.cpp:1678; ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/execution.cpp:1832; ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/execution.cpp:1851; ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/execution.cpp:2040). MatMul separates begin/commit/discard output transactions (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/dense.cpp:1222-1290).

The precise Gemmini command order inside gemmini_config_*, gemmini_mvin/mvout, gemmini_loop_ws, sp_tiled_matmul_ws and gemmini_fence is UNVERIFIED: no implementation is vendored in this tree. Phase 1 must bring those definitions under version control before claiming command-equivalent self-containment. Current source explicitly includes gemmini.h and CMake requires GEMMINI_SW_PATH (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini.cpp:41-43; ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/CMakeLists.txt:5-10). The include and boundary ledgers below enumerate the in-tree extraction seams rather than inventing external line numbers.

## PoTAL INT8: concrete linear matmul flow

1. potal_graph_compute prechecks nodes and dispatches; MUL_MAT validation is check_op/check_compute, then compute_mul_mat distinguishes raw I8, HP1 and attention contracts (ggml/src/ggml-potal/ggml-potal.cpp:311-406; ggml/src/ggml-potal/ops.cpp:58; ggml/src/ggml-potal/ops.cpp:784-816).
2. mul_mat_hp1 first selects a frozen Main tile plan using potal_tile_select, initializes activation geometry/output args, and obtains prepared weights from state.weights.get (ggml/src/ggml-potal/ops.cpp:524; ggml/src/ggml-potal/ops.cpp:582-611). Planning precedes quantization because stripe geometry changes quantization boundaries (ggml/src/ggml-potal/ops.cpp:582-599).
3. CPU preparation allocates DMA-facing operands; quantize_activations calls the shared Gemmini activation quantizer and copies codes into staged A. prepare_weight/unpack_weights call PoTAL HP1 unpack to produce codes, carrier words and channel scales (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:169-220; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:308-334). Weight cache limits are process configuration, separate from hardware-resident B reuse (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/weight_cache.cpp:23-49; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:119-130).
4. FULL: quantize_activations -> select_route -> execute_route -> run_tiled. Route reconstruction scales INT32 output by activation and channel scales, with block grouping where needed; optional FULL compensation is CPU direct execution and merge (ggml/src/ggml-potal/ops.cpp:669-695; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:348-474). reference_gemm_i32 is a CPU numeric oracle; it is not the real backend's missing-hardware fallback (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:486-499; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:902-940).
5. STRIPE_PIPELINE: mul_mat_hp1_pipeline -> run_stripe_pipeline; producer installs ExSIA StripeReadySink, quantizes and emits immutable stripe jobs, then joins the owner (ggml/src/ggml-potal/ops.cpp:313-392; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/stripe.cpp:1676-1731). stripe_run::submit/prepare_job feed the consumer; finalizer reconstructs Main/Residual and performs checked merge (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/stripe.cpp:465; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/stripe.cpp:889; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/stripe.cpp:1344-1425).
6. Tiling uses half SPAD/ACC capacity, whole scale blocks and work-ID limits. potal_tile_select chooses I/J/K and potal_frozen_plan verifies fixed plans. potal_hw_ops binds the software tiler to checked scale, loop, fence and flush functions (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/tiling.cpp:20-45; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/tiling.cpp:105-134; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/tiling.cpp:156-210).
7. Real issuer: issuer::bring_up maps/pins hart 0, flushes accelerator translations, waits for potal_fence. execute_tiled flushes/fences newly allocated buffer translations before tiled_matmul (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/execution.cpp:475-505; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/execution.cpp:609-634). The bounded I8 path explicitly runs Scale WRITE -> GEMM issue -> FENCE -> read C -> Scale RELEASE (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/execution.cpp:508-595).
8. Encoding: potal_gemm_ws_check / potal_loop_ws_check validate; potal_gemm_ws_encode / potal_loop_ws_encode create a potal_gemm_ws_program; issue_program dispatches encoded config, bounds, addresses, strides and LOOP_WS words (ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:172; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:257-307; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:316-389; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:868-899). potal_rocc.h emits .insn CUSTOM_3 with response/no-response forms and a compiler memory clobber (ggml/src/ggml-potal/include/potal_rocc.h:23-51). This is the actual instruction issue layer.
9. Completion: a successful hardware FENCE clears gemm_in_flight, then a CPU fence orders later C reads. issue_program executes a CPU fence before DMA submission. The compiler memory clobber alone is not completion (ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:847-887; ggml/src/ggml-potal/include/potal_rocc.h:11-13 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/include/potal_rocc.h:164-168). Backend native issue functions validate then return NO_HARDWARE, rather than fake success (ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:902-940).
10. Reconstruction is host work. FULL writes scaled FP32 output via reconstruct_row and execute_route; pipeline residual reconstruction uses checked wide intermediates before storing INT64 corrections and checked merge (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:79-85 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:360-432; ggml/src/ggml-potal/residual/stripe-residual.cpp:418-464 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/residual/stripe-residual.cpp:567-574). Preserve the order of saturation, radix reconstruction and FP32 conversion; changing transport must not move these arithmetic boundaries.

## Paired, indexed and resident execution

potal_make_tile_plan / potal_make_execution_plan build residencies, scale windows, index entries and operation dependencies; potal_validate_execution_plan validates them. Hardware geometry is assembled from POTAL_HW_* and fixed protocol work IDs (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:106-130; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:286; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:688; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:1588). run_paired_on_owner -> potal_paired_run_session -> potal_paired_matmul_auto stages/executes against a checked session (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:4057-4107). scale_program, issue_loop, main_gemm, index_program, residual_gemm and acc_complete are the execution-to-API boundaries (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:3223-3670).

The session supports an injected potal_paired_sink, so command recording/emulation already has a transport seam. paired_raw_untraced chooses sink or real RoCC; PAIRED_RAW wraps dispatch with optional trace and status handling (ggml/src/ggml-potal/include/potal.h:584-596; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:1154-1254). Session ownership, generation lifetime, scale release and failed-fence quarantine are execution semantics; they must not disappear when raw encoding moves into api/encode (ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:1287-1351 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/attention-hw.cpp:175-177).

H2 panel prefetch, early LdB, stream, Index Queue and counters reach separate raw-command paths, not only issue_program: queue custom functions 32-36, paired_raw_untraced, scale_column_issue, PAIRED_RAW in prefetch/stream paths, and COUNTER response calls. All are in the command-boundary ledger (ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:1173-1192; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:1581-1589; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:1812-1829; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:2390-2578; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:2847; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:3176; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:3357).

External execution semantics UNVERIFIED. |

## Threads, affinity, memory and counters

Gemmini MatmulStripeCollector starts a std::thread at ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/execution.cpp:623 and joins on shutdown/finish at ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/execution.cpp:587 and ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/matmul/execution.cpp:757. Shared ExSIA can use OpenMP tasks, or three explicit hart-role threads joined before return (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/quants/act/exsia/exsia.cpp:995-1012 and ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/quants/act/exsia/exsia.cpp:1032-1056). Thus OpenMP OFF does not mean no worker threads.

PoTAL's process issuer is a detached long-lived thread with synchronized task handoff; stripe owner start/join is a separate lifetime boundary (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/execution.cpp:343-445 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/execution.cpp:872-940). It maps /proc/cpuinfo processor/hart pairs; native test-only GGML_POTAL_HART_MAP is forbidden on RISC-V; pin_role_cpu verifies affinity (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/execution.cpp:58-150). Strict mode excludes issuer CPU from the caller and restores its prior affinity (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/execution.cpp:201-240). Stripe reconstruction runs in a separate finalizer thread with join (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/stripe.cpp:628-697); attention preserves local/fold/issuer/final reconstruction roles (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/attention.cpp:738-746 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/attention.cpp:1183-1492).

Both tensor buffers are host allocations with CPU memcpy set/get/copy. Gemmini uses ggml_aligned_malloc; PoTAL does too. HP1 operands add hardware-aware alignment and separate staged A/B/carrier/C allocations. These are ordinary virtual addresses passed to the API, not a dedicated kernel DMA allocator (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini-buffer.cpp:105-135 and ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/ggml-gemmini-buffer.cpp:187-202; ggml/src/ggml-potal/ggml-potal-buffer.cpp:46-58 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/ggml-potal-buffer.cpp:94-104; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:28-29 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:71-74 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/dense.cpp:169-220). Target page pinning/coherency beyond the visible fences and TLB flush is UNVERIFIED.

CPU rdcycle and NPU COUNTER are distinct domains. CPU scalar reader uses rdcycle on RISC-V; PoTAL COUNTER responses are uint64 values, not status codes. Paired execution configures/freezes/reads counters and records hardware windows (ggml/src/ggml-gemmini-utils/include/gemmini/cycle_reader.hpp:91-112; ggml/src/ggml-potal/include/potal_rocc.h:134-138; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:1878-1906 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/paired-auto.cpp:2037-2047). Profiling must retain both domains without calling emulator values hardware times (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:601-603).

- ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/quants/common/dequant.cpp (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:273).
- ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/quants/common/weight_reader.cpp (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:274).

- ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/residual/rmd/rmd-builder.cpp (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:276).
- ggml/src/ggml-gemmini/residual/rmd/rmd-bitmap-builder.cpp (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:277).

The generated gemmini_params.h shim includes original-potal-params.h; shared ggml_gemmini_args_t needs parameters, not command macros (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:250-260; ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/CMakeLists.txt:288-298). Include directories are backend, parent ggml/src, backend/include, Gemmini software root, generic generated-config, generated gemmini-params shim, and generated PoTAL parameters (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:352-359; ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/CMakeLists.txt:391-398). They link potal-tiled-api, potal-api, ggml-gemmini-utils and Threads; optional shared OpenMP and planning-profile library are added by the same settings function (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:365-378; ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/CMakeLists.txt:404-417).

Compile definitions reuse GGML_GEMMINI_RUNTIME_COMPILE_DEFS, block size, sigma, stripe anchor, configured PoTAL DIM, paired-auto, HARDWARE, EXSIA_VALIDATION=0, timeline hooks and ExSIA profile scope (ggml/src/ggml-potal/CMakeLists.txt:338-351; ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/CMakeLists.txt:377-390). The reusable runtime definitions carry metric flags, logs, CYCLE_SIM/CYCLE_DETAIL, compute/quantization selection, ExSIA workers/mode, RMD and operand widths (cmake/ggml-gemmini-config.cmake:54-74). INT4 also publishes POTAL_W4A4=1 through potal-api (ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/CMakeLists.txt:46-50).

## Proposed Phase 1 layer boundaries

These are audit recommendations, not changes made by Phase 0. File-level assignments in the final ledger apply to every function in that file unless overridden here.

| Existing functions / range | Proposed layer | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|
| POTAL_FUNCT_* and public command structs | api/ISA | Protocol constants live in ggml/src/ggml-potal/include/potal.h:358-381. |
| potal_*_check, potal_*_encode, encode_program | api/encode + api/validate | Pure validation/word packing precedes issue; ggml/src/ggml-potal/potal.c:139-805. |

INT4 changes are substantive: adjacent signed-nibble storage/packing (ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/include/potal-int4.h:8-63), fixed DIM32 geometry/packing identity checks (ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/CMakeLists.txt:18-44), radix-16 upper-attention residual lowering (ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/matmul/attention-residual.hpp:14-46), and W8-only tests retained as targets but disabled while independent W4 golden tests are registered (ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/tests/CMakeLists.txt:3-53). INT8 also has timeline-binary helpers absent in INT4, as shown by the file-presence ledger above. Consolidating the trees must preserve these distinct contracts.

## Hardware and API boundary occurrence ledger

Every matching source location in the Gemmini/PoTAL backend trees plus root SDK tests is recorded below. This deliberately includes function definitions/declarations, model/test calls and checked API calls as well as real issue calls: a lexical occurrence does not prove a command reaches hardware. The narrative above identifies the actual dispatch gates. PAIRED_RAW and POTAL_ROCC macro sites are included, as are plain tiled_matmul calls. Grouping by file and symbol preserves every line while avoiding repeated code. External gemmini.h expansions remain UNVERIFIED.

## Snapshot boundary during concurrent work

All inventories describe the tracked tree at 64a072afa5a7a22dec74e94d599ffa4b848c9a20. New untracked Phase 1 api/tiling files appeared during final verification and are deliberately excluded from this Phase 0 snapshot. Read-only git diff against the pinned revision found no tracked changes at that check. The baseline builder changed an untracked generated common/build-info.cpp; this audit did not modify it. These are audit observations, not claims that concurrent work is absent. External command implementation remains UNVERIFIED for the pinned tree, whose CMake requires GEMMINI_SW_PATH (cmake/ggml-gemmini-geometry.cmake:20-28).

## Additional explicit tiler and fixture issue seams

The PoTAL tiler chain is potal_tiled_matmul_auto -> potal_tiled_matmul -> potal_tiled_matmul_outer -> potal_sp_tiled_matmul_ws. The inner function writes/releases scales through ops, submits GEMM/LOOP via ops, and fences before consuming output. The stripe API prepares descriptors then runs the same planned tiler after flush/fence (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/tiling.cpp:245-345 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/tiling.cpp:425-449 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/tiling.cpp:555-600; ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/stripe-api.cpp:76-99 and ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/stripe-api.cpp:113-174). These function-pointer issue sites belong to matmul/execution above the transport implementation (ggml/src/ggml-potal/matmul/tiling.cpp:105-134).

E4B2 standalone target fixtures also issue raw custom-3 instructions directly, outside potal_rocc.h: ISSUE0/ISSUE1 use .insn and a compiler memory clobber; ordered() adds a CPU fence, and fence_potal() checks command-25 response (ggml/src/ggml-potal/tests/potal-e4b2-rocc.c:24-58; ggml/src/ggml-potal-w4a4/tests/potal-e4b2-rocc.c:24-58). Keep these isolated hardware-acceptance probes in experiment/reference and map their issue seam to api/transport for comparison. Every fixture macro occurrence follows.
